### y1v2 p.220–223 — Ch.7 sec. 7.0–7.1: waiting on the path, judging the change
[says] (p.220) "Good morning, Ayanokouji-kun."
[says] (p.220) "Yes. I was waiting for you."
[says] (p.220) "Are you an idiot?"
[says] (p.220) "I was thinking…maybe I made a mistake. The wrong choice…"
[says] (p.220) "If Sudou-kun is penalized for this, it’ll be my responsibility."
[says] (p.220) "The truth is that we’re gambling. I’m a little anxious of the outcome. Are you okay?"
[says] (p.221) "Nothing. After we settle this case,"
[says] (p.222) "What’s with her?"
[says] (p.222) "People don’t change so easily. Trying to change yourself is virtually impossible."
[trait] (p.222) The page calls it a "brief but realistic statement" that shattered the image Ayanokōji had built.
[says] (p.223) "As long as she doesn’t overdo it, I think it’ll be fine."
[says] (p.223) "If she tries to do what isn’t possible for someone like her yet, she’ll just set herself up to fail."
[trait] (p.223) The warning lands with "a mysterious yet convincing power," almost as if she were speaking from experience.
[says] (p.223) "Do you want to die for good?"

### y1v2 p.242–243 — Ch.7 sec. 7.5: the retrial, from her side
[scene] (p.242) She and Sudō arrive ten minutes before deliberation; Tachibana is already in the room, and neither her brother nor Class C has come in yet. The chapter turns to her first person here.
[says] (p.242) "I feel the same as ever."
[thinks] (p.242) The line she has committed to: "I’d declared that Sudou was completely innocent, after all. If my strategy failed, it would be for nothing."
[thinks] (p.242) "Reconciliation was another name for defeat."
[thinks] (p.242) On a failed strategy: "it would likely devolve into a verbal battle where we hurled abuse at each other."
[action] (p.242) When the doors open for her brother she turns her eyes away from him and faces the opponents who matter today.
[thinks] (p.242) "Even though I should have understood it, I felt attacked."
[thinks] (p.242) "I was wracked with various symptoms such as trembling, nervousness, and dizziness. But I couldn’t repeat yesterday’s mistakes."
[says] (p.243) "He’s not participating."
[says] (p.243) "Well, even if he’s not here, the results will be the same."
[thinks] (p.243) Her read on Chabashira’s puzzlement: "I had the gut feeling that what Chabashira-sensei was seeing was the evidence of Ayanokouji-kun’s involvement."
[thinks] (p.243) "This was a battle between truth and lies. We could bandy about ideas, but there could only be one solution."

### y1v2 p.244–245 — Ch.7 sec. 7.5: the withdrawal she did not have to ask for
[thinks] (p.244) "It wasn’t just me. Everyone else noticed that this situation was strange."
[thinks] (p.244) "It seemed that Ayanokouji-kun and Ichinose-san had handled things quite well. I tried to act calm and collected, without letting my relief show."
[action] (p.245) She grabs Sudō’s arm to stop his question and pulls him down into his seat.
[says] (p.245) "Shut up."
[thinks] (p.245) "If the complaint was withdrawn there would be no winners or losers. This was what we’d been working toward."

[says] (p.248) "What do you mean?"
[says] (p.248) "Why do you care about that?"
[says] (p.248) "I’ll leave that to your imagination."
[thinks] (p.248) "We’d fabricated a lie because we were backed into a corner."
[says] (p.249) "I don’t want to admit this, but Ayanokouji-kun…might have something exceptional to him."

[says] (p.249) "Though he does try to hide his ability by pretending to be an idiot, for some mysterious reason."
[thinks] (p.249) "Yes, he was truly incomprehensible. I could not find meaning in such behavior."
[says] (p.249) "I don’t intend to admit that I have a defect. But I do understand."

[says] (p.249) "He ‘dislikes trouble,’"

### y1v2 p.253–255 — Ch.7 sec. 7.6: the puppet question
[says] (p.253) "You’d already know, right?"
[says] (p.253) "Hey, Ayanokouji-kun. Am I just your puppet?"
[says] (p.253–254) "Ayanokouji-kun, you brought up the idea of surveillance cameras in the classrooms. Next, you took me to the special building and made me realize that there were no cameras. Then, you guided me to the idea of inventing false evidence, so that we could parse the truth from lies… When I look back on it now, that’s all I can think of."
[says] (p.254) "Who are you?" — thrown back at him after he calls her account of the case "just coincidence"
[says] (p.254) "And you are?" — to the black-haired boy who stops in front of them
[says] (p.254) "Wait. We’re not done talking, Ayanokouji-kun."

## y1v3

### y1v3 p.22–23 — Ch.2 sec. 2.0: she comes down from the ship wrong
[scene] (p.22) Deck of the liner at disembark call; Class D waits in the heat.
[status] (p.22) "Even Horikita, normally so meticulous, was concerned about her appearance. Right now, though, her hair was disheveled." — and she rubs her arms as if cold.
[says] (p.22) “I was just reading a book in my room. For Whom the Bell Tolls. You wouldn’t know it.”
[limit] (p.23) Her stated excuse for locking herself away doesn't cover what Ayanokōji saw; the chapter leaves the gap open.

### y1v3 p.33–45 — Ch.2 sec. 2.1: she asks the questions that price the test
[scene] (p.33) Class D's first rules huddle on the beach after Mashima's announcement.
[says] (p.33) “In the event that someone were to retire after we had already used all 300 points, what would happen?” — the question that establishes the floor at zero.
[rel:hirata-yousuke] (p.43–44) Stands beside Ayanokōji watching Hirata fail to decide: "Class D has no leadership, which worries me. Besides, Hirata’s a pushover. He can’t even make one decision, can he?"
[says] (p.44) “I’m the same as the others. I’ve never lived in such an environment before, so I can’t really make any predictions… What an unpleasant test.” — her first open admission that academics don't transfer here.
[rel:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.44–45) Asks him to take her side on endurance; he agrees; Classes A and B are already moving into the forest while C and D still can't start.

### y1v3 p.47–49 — Ch.2 sec. 2.2: she admits the isolation, and blocks the probe
[scene] (p.47) Walking from beach to forest; she trails the group alone.
[says] (p.47) “To be honest, I’m depressed. These kinds of things just aren’t for me. Life on an island seems primitive, and worst of all, I can’t be alone.”
[limit] (p.48) Concedes the classroom verdict against her own tools: "This test will likely fall outside of my academic abilities. I’d determined that Ike-kun and Sudou-kun would be hindrances, but they took the initiative to go out searching. I wasn’t able to do anything."
[says] (p.49) “It doesn’t feel good to have someone pry into your past” — and names Chabashira as the leak: “Chabashira-sensei leaked the information. Don’t misunderstand me. All right? Besides, we’re still not friends. Don’t forget that.”
[rel:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.49) Ends the exchange on her own terms before he can go deeper.

### y1v3 p.63–71 — Ch.2 sec. 2.4: the leader's card, and the favour she can't do herself
[scene] (p.66) The circle that picks Class D's spot-leader.
[rel:kushida-kikyou] (p.66) Kushida nominates her as the responsible choice; she accepts without visible surprise.
[says] (p.66) “I understand. I accept.” — after Sudou tries to take the role to spare her.
[feat] (p.66–67) Becomes Class D's designated leader; Hirata files her name with Chabashira and the card is handed over; the class touches the device together to hide who holds it.
[rel:sudou-ken] (p.66) His defence of her wishes is the trigger that makes her take the role rather than dodge it.
[rel:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.70–71) Delegates the Ike problem — "I'm relying on you because I know I can't do it. Can I count on you?" — then delivers the standard put-down: “I suppose it must be relaxing to be unreliable and underachieving. … Isn’t it, Ayanokouji-kun?”
[trait] (p.71) The chapter's closing read of her: alone she is "amazing…but only alone"; the school "had made her isolated life impossible."

### y1v3 p.73–74, p.92 — Ch.2 sec. 2.5 & sec. 2.8: she refuses the assistantship, and votes last
[rel:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.73) On becoming Hirata's assistant: “Me, his assistant? Don’t joke around. I’d rather dance with a mongoose.”
[limit] (p.73–74) Names her own usable condition — "If there were a clear enemy and a goal, I might be of…use" — and admits she still can't decide how far the class should spend.
[rel:kushida-kikyou] (p.92) Raises her hand for river water last, with Ayanokōji, after the whole class has already committed.

### y1v3 p.116–125 — Ch.3 sec. 3.3: the walk back, and Class B’s camp
[scene] (p.116) Walking inland from the Class C shore, she is still arguing over Ryūen's spending when the visit to Class B's camp begins.

[says] (p.122) “I was thinking that we should talk about that, too. If we didn’t have to be on guard with even one class, we’d really appreciate it. As long as you don’t mind, Ichinose-san, I’d like you to accept the proposal.”

[says] (p.124) “You can’t use any sly tricks in this test. Ryuuen has most definitely spent almost all of their points. They might be having fun right now, but they’re going to regret it later.”

[says] (p.125) “I think it’s about time to get going, Ayanokouji-kun. We’ll be a bother to Class B if we linger too long.”
[says] (p.125) “In general, I think we’re all on the same page, but they’re further ahead. I can’t deny it,”

### y1v3 p.126–129 — Ch.3 sec. 3.4: talking past Class A at the cave
[scene] (p.126) She and Ayanokōji walk up to the Class A cave together after watching from a distance fails to show anything.
[says] (p.126) “What are you planning? We gain nothing by carelessly exposing ourselves.”
[note] (p.126) The approach dialogue is untagged; the question-and-answer chain, and the question tagged to her at the end of it, put these on her.
[says] (p.127) “We came to snoop. You have a problem?”
[says] (p.127) “I mean, I would’ve thought that since you’re in Class A you’d be clever, but…”
[trait] (p.127) Switches into a deliberate provocation — the narration calls it a daring switch flipped in her brain.
[says] (p.127) “Well, rather than clever, I’d say you’re underhanded. What cowardly methods.”
[says] (p.127) “I’m Horikita, from Class D.”
[says] (p.127) “Stupid, huh? In that case, there’s no harm in us seeing what’s inside, right? Or does that make you feel uncomfortable?”
[says] (p.127) “So it’s not a problem if we look? You’re in the way.”
[says] (p.127) “We’re just going to look inside. That itself isn’t a violation of the rules, right?”
[says] (p.127) “Oh? So you occupy this place. I didn’t know that. Is there a device inside?”
[rel:totsuka-yahiko] (p.127–129) Provokes him on purpose and talks past every block he puts up.
[says] (p.128) “Well, there definitely aren’t any rules that say we can’t go into the cave. We should have the right to look inside, or at least to verify if there’s equipment, right? If we couldn’t do that, then people could just forcibly monopolize every spot. That’s not what this test is about.”
[feat] (p.128) The monopoly argument: occupation bars use, not looking, and a spot that nobody may inspect could be seized by anyone.
[says] (p.128) “You’re forcefully monopolizing control of the spot. The rules don’t protect such actions.”
[note] (p.128) Both p.128 lines are untagged; the second is answered by Katsuragi conceding the point.
[rel:katsuragi-kouhei] (p.128–129) Matched and stopped by his unspoken-rule argument.
[says] (p.129) “Well, fine. I look forward to seeing the results, and Class A’s abilities.”
[limit] (p.129) Leaves with the wind knocked out of her; without Katsuragi's arrival she would have gone through the vinyl.

### y1v3 p.171–178 — Ch.6 sec. 6.1: the objection
[scene] (p.172) Breaks the girls’ unanimity by objecting to Karuizawa, distrusting Hirata as much as the other boys.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.172) “Wait a minute. I’d like to raise an objection—especially against you, Karuizawa-san.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.172) “I don’t particularly mind dividing up the living areas for men and women. As long as the culprit hasn’t been found, it is certainly a good idea to keep our distance from the men, considering the likelihood that the culprit is among them. However, I don’t trust Hirata-kun. I cannot dismiss the possibility that he might be the underwear thief. Also, I’m unconvinced that he should be excluded from the ban on men.”
[note] (p.173) “That’s just your personal belief, isn’t it? Don’t force your way of thinking on me.” is left untagged in the translation; it sits between Karuizawa’s two lines about Hirata and is placed here as her reply.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.173) “Don’t make me repeat myself. Nothing you can say will convince me.”
[note] (p.173) “I won’t speak impulsively. Simply put, I would be fine if you increased the number by one more guy. If you did, they’d be effective in watching each other’s backs.” is left untagged in the translation; placed here as her answer.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.173) “Might this be happening due to your naïve handling of crisis management? Maybe there is an ulterior motive for stealing the underwear that we don’t yet understand.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.173) “I don’t care that your underwear was stolen. That kind of thing happens every day, and there’s nothing you can really do about it. It’s likely someone here bears a grudge against you.”
[inference] (p.173) She is considering that the culprit’s goal was not the underwear itself but humiliating Karuizawa.
[trait] (p.173) Socializing is her weak point: smart, but with difficulty relating to other people.
[rel:karuizawa-kei] (p.173) The public clash: Karuizawa calls her clueless about boyfriends, and she calls the crisis management naïve.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.174) “I agree with you. Considering how these three behave on a daily basis, they are completely untrustworthy. I’ve thought it over very carefully, and I intend to choose someone who can’t be the culprit.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.174) “You. Ayanokouji-kun.”
[rel:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.174) Nominates him as the one other boy the girls can trust, over Karuizawa’s objections.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.176) “Is it okay for us to continue the discussion now? This farce is a waste of time.”
[trait] (p.176) Silently watches Karuizawa’s excoriation of Sakura, then sighs and brushes a hand through her hair as if she’d observed something completely boring.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.177) “You’re naïve, Karuizawa-san.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.177) “It’s certainly true that Ayanokouji-kun has many unlikable qualities,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.177) “But we need to ask if Hirata-kun can trust Ayanokouji-kun. It would only be awkward and uncomfortable if you endorsed someone meaningless to Hirata-kun. The truth is, there is not one thing that I trust about him, but I have no intention of inserting my own personal feelings into the matter. By process of elimination, I’ve concluded that he is the most trustworthy boy in the class. Or is another boy in our class preferable? If there is, I’d like for you to tell me.”
[trait] (p.177) The other girls treat her poorly; both sides ignore each other, and living together now makes a clash of tempers inevitable.

### y1v3 p.184–189 — Ch.6 sec. 6.3: points and the fever
[scene] (p.184) Pulls Ayanokōji into the girls’ tent to show what the class has been spending, then proposes a partnership against the split.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.184) “It must be really tough, being forced to do routine chores,”

[says:horikita-suzune] (p.187) “I’m trying to understand you… Do you have some other intention?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.187) “Well, whatever. It’s settled then.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.187) “You have such a serious look on your face. Do you really hate being treated like a criminal that much?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.187) “Our cooperation was a complete coincidence. Class D didn’t really have any sense of teamwork to begin with. It’s ending rather badly, though, especially because of the broken trust between the guys and girls. Of course it would’ve been better to stay united until the end of the test.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.187) “Let’s not get ahead of ourselves… Sorry, but I’m going back into the tent.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.187) “Confessed? Confessed what?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.188) “Not really any more than usual.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.188) “When…did you notice?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.188) “Yeah, and I told you I’d been reading in my room.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.188) “And on what evidence do you base that conclusion?”
[status] (p.188) A fever close to 38°C, hidden since the ship; reporting illness would hit the class with a bigger penalty.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.188) “I just need to hang in there for another few days. If I give up now, it’ll … all be for nothing. Goodnight.”
[trait] (p.189) She intends to soldier through to the bitter end — an iron will.

### y1v3 p.191–199 — Ch.6 sec. 6.4: search in pairs
[scene] (p.192) Overhears Kushida’s excuse for being left behind, then takes the lead on the last forage.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.192) “Why were you left behind? What about your usual group of friends?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.193) “Even if she’s not feeling well, it’s a natural physiological phenomenon. She should be fine. I suppose that’s to be expected, though. However, why did you deliberately choose our group? You must have had other options available.”
[rel:kushida-kikyou] (p.193) Grills her for choosing this group — she detests Kushida, and Kushida knows it.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.193) “I don’t have enough free time to spend it with you unnecessarily.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.196) “You got too close. Remember to stay out of my personal space.”
[action] (p.196) Slaps Yamauchi when he peers into her face from a centimetre away.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.197) “For the time being, let’s begin with the area,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.197) “How long do you want to travel? We need to search for food, right?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.197) “Don’t make any moves alone. Search in pairs. Be especially careful. Let’s go, Ayanokouji-kun.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.198) “I always have it on me.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.198) “When we update the device, I’ll slip in among the students Hirata-kun’s gathered so I can be undetected. Ibuki-san and the other students aren’t supposed to know.”
[rel:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.198) Hands over her leader card for him to inspect away from base camp.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.198) “Maybe, but what do you plan to do after I show you?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.198) “Er, right. If you have proof, that would be a huge achievement.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.198) “Well? Is this the same card that Katsuragi-kun had?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.199) “The keycards might have a different color scheme based on the class.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.199) “Yeah, but we don’t have enough evidence to make a solid judgment. If we make a mistake, we won’t recover from it.”
[action] (p.199) Snatches the dropped card from the mud, then keeps her distance from him in fury.

### y1v3 p.200–202 — Ch.6 sec. 6.5: thrown, then washed
[scene] (p.200) Ill and distracted, she does not see Yamauchi coming until mud goes over her hair — she throws him without a word.
[says:yamauchi-haruki] (p.200) “Ha ha ha ha! You’re all covered in mud, Horikita! Hilarious!”
[action] (p.200) Grabs his pointing arm and throws him the moment he laughs.
[rel:yamauchi-haruki] (p.201) Takes his lifetime-grudge line for the mud; he cowers behind Ayanokōji.
[says] (p.201) “I’m going to hold a grudge against you for the rest of my life. You’d best prepare yourself.”
[says] (p.201) “Yeah… This situation is rather painful.”
[says] (p.201) “Right. Looks like I don’t have any other choice.”
[action] (p.201) With the shower line blocked by Karuizawa’s group, she takes the river instead.
[action] (p.202) Washes the mud off in the cold river while sick, then appears in a swimsuit with Kushida.

### y1v3 p.203–205 — Ch.6 sec. 6.6: the confession
[scene] (p.203) She draws Ayanokōji into the forest and tells him the key card was stolen.
[says] (p.203) “Ayanokouji-kun. Could you please come here for a moment?”
[says] (p.203) “Follow me. We can’t talk here.”
[says] (p.203) “This happened because of my carelessness. I am aware that I made a mistake. Okay?”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.203) “A mistake?”
[says] (p.203) “It was stolen.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.203) “W-wait, your underwear was stolen, too?”
[says] (p.203) “No. This is much, much worse. The…key card. A complete blunder on my part.”
[says] (p.203) “I wanted to talk to you because I trust you. I absolutely couldn’t consult with someone who might be the culprit. This is so humiliating. I want… to die…”
[says] (p.204) “Such a huge failure.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.204) “No, the one who stole it is to blame. Right?”
[says] (p.204) “Even so, this is an issue of responsibility. It has nothing to do with me being sick or covered in mud.”
[says] (p.204) “I wasn’t supposed to let go of the card for a single second. But I…”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.204) “Don’t blame yourself. I doubt this’ll be any consolation, but I think you did your absolute best.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.204) “It’s probably better if we don’t make this information public. We should get to the truth first.”
[says] (p.204) “Yes. I think so, too.”
[says] (p.204) “I suspect two people. Either Karuizawa-san, or Ibuki-san.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.204) “Unfortunately, the chances of that are low. Karuizawa was in front of the shower the whole time.”
[says] (p.204) “You’re sure about that?”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.204) “Yeah. Same goes for her flunkies, too.”
[says] (p.204) “If that’s the case, it’s highly likely Ibuki-san is the culprit. It’s possible she came to know about the card this morning, and the timing is just far too good.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.205) “If we examine the timing and talk with Ibuki, we can understand what happened. If we suspect Ibuki, we shouldn’t take our eyes off of her. Her escape would probably be the worst-case scenario, right?”
[says] (p.205) “That’s right. I’m sorry, but can you go back to camp first? That way you can start following her immediately.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.205) “Sure. I understand. I’ll keep an eye on her.”
[action] (p.205) Sent back to camp alone, likely wanting room to be sick by herself.

### y1v3 p.206–208 — Ch.6 sec. 6.7: the fire from her side
[scene] (p.206) Returns to a smoke-filled camp and turns the fire straight onto Ibuki.
[says] (p.206) “I can’t see Ibuki. The fire might have been her handiwork. Where is she?”
[rel:ibuki-mio] (p.206) Ready to call out to Ibuki at the fire, but hesitates when she sees her genuine confusion.
[says] (p.206) “Did the manual get burned?”
[says] (p.206) “It’s just one thing after another…”
[action] (p.212) Gone from the camp by the time the luggage is secured — she has set out after Ibuki.

### y1v3 p.213–220 — Ch.6 sec. 6.8: the pursuit
[scene] (p.213) Her viewpoint: she chases Ibuki’s footprints through the downpour, sick and alone, until Ibuki waits for her as if expecting her.
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.213) “What are you doing, Horikita?”
[says] (p.213) “When did you notice me?”
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.213) “Right from the start.”
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.213) “Why were you following me?”
[says] (p.213) “Do you really not know?”
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.213) “No, I don’t know.”
[says] (p.213) “You clearly know why I was following you, don’t you?”
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.213) “I really have no idea what you’re talking about.”
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.213) “Why should I lie?”
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.213) “I’d at least like to hear why you followed me, from your own lips.”
[says] (p.214) “The underwear theft and the fire. Misfortune continues to befall Class D.”
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.214) “So what?”
[says] (p.214) “Do you realize that some people suspect you?”
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.214) “Ah. I suppose since I’m an outsider, there’s not much I can do about that.”
[says] (p.214) “That’s what I’m talking about.”
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.214) “You’re saying I’m the culprit? Do you have any proof?”
[says] (p.214) “Unfortunately, I don’t have a single shred of evidence related to the underwear theft. But I think it was you.”
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.214) “That’s a pretty terrible thing to say. You don’t have any evidence, yet you suspect me?”
[says] (p.214) “I suspect you because of today. You don’t need me to explain that, do you?”
[says] (p.214) “Let me cut to the chase. I want you to return something you took from me,”
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.214) “I don’t know what you’re talking about.”
[says] (p.214) “Where are you going?”
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.214) “Who knows?”
[thinks] (p.215) “I have to make up for my mistake. I have to make up for my mistake.”
[thinks] (p.215) “I can handle it well. I can do it. I can do it.”
[limit] (p.216) The fever peaks in the downpour; she can no longer hold her head up.
[says] (p.216) “Ibuki-san. I’m going to investigate you with everything I’ve got. You don’t mind?”
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.216) “With everything you’ve got? Could you be a bit more specific? You mean you’re going to resort to violence?”
[says] (p.216) “This is your last warning. Return it…”
[back] (p.216) She recalls condemning Sudou after his Class C trial — and abandoning him — now that she is considering the same violence.
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.217) “Oh, you’re good,”
[says] (p.217) “An act of violence means immediate disqualification…”
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.217) “Someone might see us here, you mean? Weren’t you willing to use violence, too?”
[action] (p.217) Thrown into the mud; she slips Ibuki’s grip, rolls away, and rises — the first real thanks her martial arts practice gets her.
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.217) “Oh? You can really move, surprisingly. You practice or something?”
[says] (p.217) “I’ve…been nothing but a complete and utter failure in this test.”
[thinks] (p.217) “I mocked people. I hated useless things, labeling them incompetent, while I was useless myself.”
[says] (p.217) “It was you, wasn’t it? You stole the keycard.”
[feat] (p.218) Meets Ibuki’s trained footwork in a back-and-forth exchange — no hesitation on either side to hurt.
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.218) “You’ve been trying really hard until now. I’ll tell you the truth, as a reward. I stole the card.”
[action] (p.218) Watches Ibuki draw the card out and show the engraved name.
[says] (p.218) “You gave up the truth pretty easily.”
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.218) “It doesn’t matter if I admit it or not now. There’s no evidence I used violence against you. It’s not as though the school can make a judgment here. Isn’t that right?”
[status] (p.218) The card is back in Ibuki’s pocket; fingerprints on it are her only argument for recovering it.
[feat] (p.219) Drives her left fist into Ibuki’s solar plexus with her last strength — it pins her but does not knock her down.
[says] (p.219) “This is the worst… I’m already… At my limit…”
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.219) “I don’t understand… I thought you were involved.”
[says] (p.219) “Involved? In what?”
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.219) “I didn’t burn the manual.”
[says] (p.219) “You intend to keep lying even now?”
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.219) “What would I gain by burning it? It was inevitable that people would begin searching for the criminal after the uproar. Besides, people would suspect me rather strongly. There was nothing to gain and a lot to lose.”
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.220) “Do you think he’s in Class D? There’s a guy who figured me out before you did.”
[note] (p.220) Ibuki leaves the man’s name out; the page names no one.
[says] (p.220) “So. You can’t mean…”
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.220) “This conversation is over.”
[action] (p.220) Knocked out cold by a blunt strike to the head as the talk “ends”.
[says] (p.220) “Do you think they’ll permit such coercive methods?”
[says:ibuki-mio] (p.220) “Come on, now. I don’t feel like answering that.”
[thinks] (p.220) “By attempting to fix that mistake myself, I ended up turning it into a situation that couldn’t be fixed.”

### y1v3 p.229–235 — Ch.6 sec. 6.10: in his arms
[scene] (p.229) Wakes in his arms under the tree, fever high, and pieces the day together before choosing to go on alone.
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.229) “Are you awake?”
[says] (p.229) “Ayano…kouji-kun?”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.229) “You have a high fever. Don’t push yourself.”
[says] (p.229) “I knew it…Ibuki-san stole my card.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.229) “This isn’t a test where you can just hide for twenty-four hours a day, right? No matter what you do, you can be open to attack.”
[says] (p.229) “I could’ve avoided this if I knew how to rely on someone…”
[says] (p.229) “When I was losing consciousness, I felt like I could hear Ryuuen’s voice… It’s strange, I thought he’d already retired…”
[rel:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.229) Half-convinced she heard his voice while unconscious, though she took him for retired.
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.229) “You were losing consciousness. Maybe you had a dream?”
[says] (p.229) “If it was a dream, it was a nightmare…”
[says] (p.230) “I’m sorry…”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.230) “Why are you apologizing to me?”
[says] (p.230) “There’s no one I can apologize to except for you…”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.230) “If you think things are bad, then make some reliable friends. Start there first.”
[says] (p.230) “That’s difficult advice… No one would want to be with me.”
[says] (p.230) “It’s unpleasant to be made fun of, though…”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.230) “It’s just that you’ve started to sound like you need allies.”
[says] (p.230) “Nobody would say that…”
[says] (p.230) “I should have understood this a long time ago…”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.230) “Don’t talk. You’re sick.”
[says] (p.231) “I’m going to try to get up to Class A using my own abilities. I’ll definitely recover from this failure…”
[says] (p.231) “I’m prepared to be hated by everyone else… This was all my mistake.”
[goal] (p.231) States her goal out loud: Class A on her own abilities, for her brother to acknowledge her.
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.231) “According to this school’s system, if you fight by yourself, you won’t reach Class A. We need to cooperate with our classmates. It’s unavoidable.”
[says] (p.231) “I can’t accept that. No matter how hard it is, I’m still…alone.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.231) “Ah, shut up already! Stop talking. Right now, you won’t be able to convince anyone.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.231) “You can’t bear every responsibility. You’re not that strong, unfortunately.”
[says] (p.231) “So you’re telling me to give up? I have a dream to reach Class A, a dream for my brother to acknowledge me.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.231) “No one said you have to give up.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.231) “If you can’t fight by yourself, it’s better to fight with a partner. I’ll lend you a hand.”
[says] (p.231) “Why? You’re not the kind of person who would say such things…”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.231) “Well, why then? I wonder.”
[voice] (p.231) Grabs his sleeve while she appeals — her grip is weak but he still feels it.
[says] (p.233) “I can’t do that. We can’t afford to lose thirty points because of me… I was the one who confronted Karuizawa-san and the others about using points, right? It would make me look like such an idiot…”
[says] (p.233) “Not just that… The keycard was stolen from me, too. You understand what that means?”
[says] (p.233) “Just leave me here and head back. If you do, I’ll be the only one absent from roll call.”
[says] (p.233) “What are you planning to do?”
[says] (p.233) “By tomorrow morning, I’ll…try to return by myself, somehow. If I can deal with my poor health during roll call, then I’ll do something about retirement.”
[says] (p.233) “Still, there’s nothing else I can do…”
[action] (p.233) Covers her eyes with her arm to hide the tears while he works out the points.
[says] (p.234) “Yes. That’s the correct decision. Everything was my responsibility.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.234) “Can you really make it back alone by tomorrow morning?”
[says] (p.234) “Yeah… I’ll be fine.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.234) “Horikita. Do you really think that not retiring is the right decision?”
[says] (p.234) “Of course I do. Retiring isn’t an option for me.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.234) “Hey. Why do you think that we’ve been backed into such a corner?”
[says] (p.234) “I failed due to my negligence. That’s all.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.234) “You’re wrong. You’re completely wrong.”
[says] (p.234) “Go… Because I think of you as my friend, please listen to my request…”
[note] (p.234) She calls him a friend, then covers her mouth in surprise at her own words.
[says] (p.235) “I’m going to make this right… As if it didn’t happen at all.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.235) “No, this is the wrong choice.”
[note] (p.235) The reply is untagged in the translation; it answers her plan to go on alone, so it is recorded as his.
[says] (p.235) “It’s fine. I can… By myself… Ugh…”
[says] (p.235) “Go, please…”
[action] (p.235) Loses consciousness again while he holds her.
[status] (p.237) Carried unconscious to the ship and retires with a high fever at 7:58, minutes before roll call.

### y1v3 p.245–247 — Ch.7: the deck and the apology
[scene] (p.245) Returns to the class on the ship’s deck still pale after her retirement; Sudou greets her, she takes back the first-name permission, and Karuizawa apologizes to her face.
[says] (p.246) “I’m not too bad. I can’t say that I’m back to full health. More than anything else, retiring was a big mistake on my part.”
[says:sudou-ken] (p.246) “Don’t worry about it.”
[rel:sudou-ken] (p.246) Accepts the first name for one line, then withdraws the permission.
[says] (p.246) “By the way, Sudou-kun. Don’t just go around calling me by my first name without permission. Understand?”
[says:sudou-ken] (p.246) “Y-y-yeah.”
[note] (p.246) “But…what happened? Why was Class D ranked first?” runs untagged before Karuizawa’s tagged line and is left out as a chapter attribution gap.
[says:hirata-yousuke] (p.246) “Well, Karuizawa-san, I think you should talk to Horikita-san first, don’t you agree?”
[says:hirata-yousuke] (p.246) “Horikita-san, do you have a minute?”
[says] (p.246) “Yes. There’s something that we’re supposed to talk about. Right?”
[rel:karuizawa-kei] (p.246) Faces her to eat humble pie after the accusation about the points.
[says:karuizawa-kei] (p.246) “I’m sorry.”
[says:karuizawa-kei] (p.246) “Ibuki-san stole my underwear. Ayanokouji-kun told us everything.”
[says] (p.246) “Huh?”
[note] (p.246) The one-word reply follows the narration that she was puzzled; recorded as hers.
[says:karuizawa-kei] (p.247) “Horikita-san, when you noticed that Ibuki-san was the culprit, she tried to run because you questioned her, right? That’s why you ended up collapsing and getting sick…”
[says:karuizawa-kei] (p.247) “I heard about it from Hirata-kun first. He said that you found out the leaders of Class A and C. That’s why we had so many points. So, I’m…sorry for everything I said.”
[says] (p.247) “Wait a minute. I… You said I found out the leaders’ identities? But I retired¾”
[says] (p.247) “Wait. Ayanokouji-kun, what did you¾”
[note] (p.247) Both replies keep the printed three-quarter marks. The classmates’ praise lines — “There’s no need for you to be humble…”, “Horikita-san, you were so incredibly awesome!…”, “When I heard that you’d retired…” — run untagged from unnamed speakers and are left out as chapter attribution gaps.
[says] (p.247) “W-wait a second!”
[action] (p.247) Is buried in the crowd’s congratulations while he withdraws, and shouts after him from inside it.

### y1v3 p.250–258 — Ch.7 sec. 7.1: the whole explanation
[scene] (p.250) Section 7.1 opens with her storming the lounge for the full story; he gives it on one condition — she never investigates him.
[says] (p.250) “What do these test results mean? What in the world happened?”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.250) “You look like you don’t have any idea at all.”
[says] (p.250) “I just can’t imagine it. I don’t understand it at all. I have a mountain of questions.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.250) “I’ll tell you everything. However, my one condition is that you stay quiet on this matter. I won’t compromise on this.”
[rel:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.250) Sets the price of the story: her silence, with no compromise.
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.250) “What would you like to ask?”
[says] (p.250) “What were you doing during the test? Tell me,” she said.
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.250) “When the special test was announced, I didn’t focus on anything except for the additional rules. I roughly understood how to manage the 300 points, but you couldn’t manipulate them as an individual.”
[says] (p.250) “But the additional rules were really difficult to understand. If you did things normally, you wouldn’t have been able to identify the leaders. Right?”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.250) “Yeah. First of all, I volunteered to join the search for the base camp. Able to move freely, I planned to search out spot locations ahead of everyone else.”
[says] (p.251) “You make it sound simple, but no one should have known the spot locations.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.251) “That’s not true. You didn’t understand because you were ill and holed up inside the ship, but the school already gave us hints about the locations when we were sailing around the island.”
[fact] (p.251) The circling ship — nearly three times a usual cruise’s speed — and the phrase “significant scenery” were the school’s hints about the spot locations; Katsuragi and Kōenji had noticed the same hint.
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.251) “Then, I reached the cave. I thought it was the most important base.”
[says] (p.251) “The cave was the most important base? Don’t you think that the river and the well would have been more convenient, though?”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.251) “The spot itself wasn’t what was important. Its location was.”
[says] (p.251) “But what advantage is there in going into the cave if you don’t have a keycard?”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.251) “Well, I’d intended to explore various things, but in the end I discovered the leader’s identity.”
[says] (p.251) “Well, I suppose that Katsuragi-kun was careless.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.251) “No, that wasn’t it.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.251) “There was that guy, Yahiko, remember? The one following Katsuragi around? He was the leader. I saw Katsuragi and Yahiko at the cave, but I didn’t see the moment they occupied it. After the two of them walked away, I checked if the cave was occupied or not.”
[fact] (p.251) Totsuka Yahiko was Class A’s leader; Katsuragi only displayed the card while the two were at the cave.
[says] (p.252) “Wouldn’t you have mistaken Katsuragi-kun as the leader?” she asked.
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.252) “Do you think that the leader would have shown off the card so carelessly in front of people?”
[says] (p.252) “But why? Why bother deliberately holding onto the card, then?”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.252) “Because he didn’t have any other choice. As far as I can tell, Katsuragi is a calm and collected man, excessively cautious. There’s no way he didn’t understand the high risk of occupying a spot immediately after finding it. In other words, the person who occupied it was enticed by shortsighted greed.”
[says] (p.252) “That’s…why there was another person.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.252) “Yes.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.252) “So, aside from their base, Class A held down at least two spots, but we didn’t confirm how many they were occupying by the end of the test. If I correctly guessed the identity of their leader, though, I could invalidate all of their points.”
[says] (p.252) “I’m still not convinced. If he figured out the spot’s location at such an early stage, and if he were acting alongside a lot of other people, shouldn’t he have avoided trouble? Even if he just had someone standing watch by the cave, it should have been sufficient claim of ownership. Why would they occupy it?”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.253) “That was probably Class A’s disadvantage.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.253) “Their class appears perfect at first glance, but it actually has a huge schism in it.”
[fact] (p.253) Class A’s internal schism is the reason Katsuragi could not rely on other people — why the methods revealed Class A so easily.
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.253) “That’s why I excluded Class A at that stage, and turned my attention toward Class C. Katsuragi was easy to understand, but with Ryuuen, there were many unknown variables. To tell you the truth, he was gathering more information than I was. He’d found out the identities of all of the leaders.”
[says] (p.253) “W-wait, he found out the identities of all of the leaders… So not only Class D, but also the leaders for Classes B and A? But that’s strange. We were far from being penalized; we ended up getting first place by a wide margin. How do you intend to explain that?”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.253) “This is a little difficult to explain, but the reason I made you retire is the answer.”
[says] (p.253) “Wait, the answer is you made me retire? What in the world did you do?”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.253) “Oh, that reminds me. I didn’t return it to the school yet.”
[action] (p.253) Takes the single card he hands her; the engraved name reads “Ayanokouji Kiyotaka.”
[says] (p.253) “This is a keycard. Why do you…?!”
[says] (p.253) “Wait, why does…?”
[fact] (p.253) The leader card in his pocket bears his own name.
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.253) “The test had to be fair. The rules were fundamentally created to be fair.”
[says] (p.254) “That’s… The leader would be absent. So the rights of exclusive possession would disappear…”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.254) “Wrong. In the manual, it says, ‘It is impossible to change the leader without suitable justification.’ Don’t you think that retirement is suitable enough justification?”
[rule] (p.254) The manual’s leader-change clause — retirement is suitable justification; the additional rules were made to be broken when someone is out through poor health or injury, with the base-camp clause as the parallel case.
[says] (p.254) “So then, you made me…?”
[fact] (p.254) Horikita Suzune retired and he was appointed in her place — the leader the other classes were supposed to guess at the end, there can only be one.
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.254) “That’s why even though Class C knew you were the leader, we avoided penalties.”
[says] (p.254) “But wait. Ibuki-san stole my card, but what if I’d protected it really thoroughly?”
[says] (p.254) “Did you drop the card on purpose back then? Well, I suppose that Yamauchi-kun’s actions might have provided Ibuki-san the opportunity to hatch a plan to steal the keycard…”
[rel:yamauchi-haruki] (p.254) The mud bribe she took read back as the opening that let Ibuki take the card.
[says] (p.254) “Unless I knew what Ibuki-san was aiming for right at the start, I couldn’t have done anything…”
[fact] (p.255) Ibuki was picked up by Class D, but not by chance — the near-coincidence of two people saved by two separate classes dissolved once Kaneda in Class B came out as a spy Ryuuen had sent.
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.255) “Besides, Ibuki has a habit of looking people in the eye when she’s lying.”
[says] (p.255) “Wait, when she lied she would look the person in the eye? Isn’t it usually the opposite?”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.255) “Generally speaking, you avoid eye contact if you have a guilty conscience. However, it was the opposite for her. I think that she made eye contact to make the person think that the lie was the truth. She probably didn’t even notice it herself.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.255) “Her goal was probably to find the keycard, but she might have intended to disrupt Class D at the same time.”
[says] (p.255) “But I have to wonder why Ibuki-san specifically stole my keycard. All she needed was to check my name.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.255) “That was likely Ibuki’s intention from the start. However, she ran into unexpected trouble.”
[fact] (p.255) Ibuki carried a digital camera to take a picture of the keycard; a photo would have made the leader’s identity clear to anyone and let her profit.
[says] (p.255) “To take a picture…with the camera? Why would she go so far?”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.255) “If she had a picture, then the leader’s identity would have been clear for anyone to see, right? If she had convincing evidence, she would have profited.”
[says] (p.255) “I don’t quite understand… Did Ryuuen-kun not trust Ibuki-san?”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.255) “That wasn’t it.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.256) “From here on out, I don’t have any evidence of what I’m saying. Think of it as my intuition, which I derived from the test results. At the end of the test, Class A had 270 points.”
[inference] (p.256) His own label: intuition from the results, with no evidence — Class A ended the test with 270 points and had not used a single one.
[fact] (p.256) Classes A and C were connected behind the scenes: Class C sacrificed its own points and bought whatever Class A needed, and by taking all of C’s tools Class A spent the week without using any points — Ibuki’s evidence went to someone in Class A.
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.256) “By the way, I became aware of Class C’s leader after half of the students retired. It was certain that the leader would have remained on the island, right?”
[says] (p.256) “Even so, we weren’t supposed to know who was left.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.256) “No, I was almost one hundred percent sure that Ryuuen was still on the island.”
[fact] (p.256) Ibuki hiding a wireless transceiver in the ground proved someone was left on the island for her to contact; Ryuuen set it on a table unguarded while on vacation — no one else controlled it, and his mistake was trusting no one.
[says] (p.257) “My god… I don’t even have the words,” Horikita answered, facing the truth.
[fact] (p.257) The test in one line: Class A failed on its internal rift, Class B ran defense-only and paid for believing Kaneda, Class C sent spies and negotiated with A, and Class D avoided damage because he had been appointed the leader.
[says] (p.257) “I don’t like this. You completely used me, like a pawn.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.257) “Yeah. I can’t deny that. I wouldn’t be surprised if you never want to speak to me ever again.”
[rel:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.257) Accepts the charge without defense; he was aware of what he had done.
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.257) “Well, I’ll be heading back to my room now. I’m really tired,” I said.
[says] (p.257) “Wait. We’re not done talking.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.257) “What? I just want to relax in my room, if possible.”
[says] (p.257) “After you explain everything. There are still some things we need to talk about, right?”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.257) “Well…like what?”
[says] (p.257) “The reason why you participated in this special test. Was it to fight alone? I don’t care that you used me this time. I want to know why you put in the effort when you dislike trouble.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.257) “I wonder.”
[rel:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.257) Her real question is his principles, not the test — the explanation so far was the less important part to her.
[says] (p.257) “I don’t have any room for doubt. I understand your talents now. If you help me, aiming for Class A seems a realistic enough goal. But what are your principles? Why did you do this?”
[goal] (p.257) Names her own goal with him in it: Class A, realistically, if his principles hold.
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.257) “Because I was touched that you tried to fight all alone when you were ill.”
[says] (p.258) “You wouldn’t normally say that kind of thing. It’s easy to spot the lie.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.258) “Well, what I mean is I don’t feel like explaining it.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.258) “I don’t mind helping you get up to Class A. However, I have one condition. Do not investigate me. If you promise not to touch on this subject ever again, I will help you.”
[rel:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.258) The deal is struck: his help toward Class A in exchange for his past staying buried.
[says] (p.258) “If you don’t want to talk, there’s nothing I can really do about that. If you’ll help, I have no reason to refuse you. I’m not interested in digging things up that are better left buried. After all, you dislike trouble.”
[rel:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.258) Takes his hand without hesitation; the handshake is firm — “I work for me. You do things for yourself.” — and the battle to raise the class from the bottom begins.

### y1v4 p.13, p.15–16, p.20 — Ch.2: the three, the thief, the friend-type

### y1v4 p.30–31 — Ch.2 sec. 2.2: the message chat
[scene] (p.30) Chat only: Ayanokōji messages her about the school’s test notice; she replies instantly.
[says] (p.30) “Yeah. I did.” (chat reply; the translation leaves the thread’s lines untagged)
[says] (p.30) “Mine says 20:40. That seems like a pretty big difference.” (chat)
[says] (p.31) “Because your time is earlier, I look forward to hearing your report.” (chat)
[trait] (p.30) Replies instantly — against her usual half-day, sometimes days, reading lag.
[note] (pp.30–31) Two further chat lines sit unassigned in the translation: “I’m curious about the difference in start times…” and “We can’t know anything for sure at this point.” The volume attribution list keeps them.
[note] (pp.17–19) Off-page: Hirata’s whole lunch ask is about winning her over, routed through Ayanokōji.
[rel:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (pp.30–31) The chat partner with the earlier slot; she defers his report.

### y1v4 p.51, p.55–61 — Ch.2 sec. 2.5: the Katsuragi warning and the cold exit
[scene] (p.51) Off-screen first — Ayanokōji reports the Rabbit rules to her by chat — then on the second deck she faces Katsuragi, holds against Kanzaki’s interlude, and turns her back on Ryūen.
[note] (p.51) Receives his chat report on the Rabbit group’s rules; her reply is not printed.
[says] (p.55) “Yes, I am. And what exactly does that have to do with you?” (to Katsuragi)
[says] (p.55) “You wanted to speak with me? Funny. Didn’t you completely ignore me the other day?”
[says] (p.56) “That’s rather petty. From Class A’s perspective, are we such a big deal? I mean, there’s a significant gap in points between us.”
[says] (p.56) “You needn’t concern yourself. Class D has always been regarded as inferior. I’d welcome any change in our reputation.”
[says] (p.57) “What do you mean?” (when Katsuragi calls the island win luck)
[says] (p.57) “We haven’t gotten into college yet. I don’t think you and I are so different. The school arbitrarily divided us into different classes. Don’t forget that.”
[says] (p.60) “If we’re going to continue this pointless conversation, I’d prefer to just go inside. Besides, it’s about time.” (flipping her hair away from Ryūen)
[trait] (pp.55–61) Doesn’t back down before the Class A entourage; the narration credits her — weak-willed students would ingratiate themselves, she acts as she always does.
[trait] (p.56) Reads her own new reputation: other classes now treat her selfishness as class concern and fear her as a hidden mastermind.
[rel:katsuragi-kouhei] (pp.55–57) The pivot from his island-test indifference to a Class A threat.
[rel:kanzaki-ryuuji] (p.56) Declines his rescue — “You needn’t concern yourself.”
[rel:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.60) Exits on him mid-provocation.
[rel:hirata-yousuke] (p.54) Both summoned to the 20:40 meeting (see Hirata’s row).

### y1v4 p.52–53 — Ch.2: summoned, and the smarts check

### y1v4 p.63–77 — Ch.3 sec. 3.0: the Blue Ocean briefing
[scene] (p.63) Meets Ayanokōji at the empty Blue Ocean café an hour early, hands over her memorized Dragon group list at 8:00, and holds the table through Ryūen’s interrupt.
[says] (p.63) “You’re rather early.”
[says] (p.63) “That’s not my problem, as I’ve still arrived before the designated time. Besides, how would I know if you’d been waiting even ten hours or more?”
[trait] (p.63) The narrator calls her one of the very few people at this school he could call a friend — and a thorn because she knows something about his hidden life.
[action] (p.64) Hands Ayanokōji a slip of paper: the Dragon roster she wrote and memorized, biased “to the point where I can’t imagine it’s a coincidence.”
[says] (p.64) “You mean Ichinose-san being in your group, don’t you? I suppose only the Class B students know if she’s really excellent or not. A leader’s qualities and level of excellence aren’t necessarily directly proportional,” said Horikita.
[says] (p.65) “From what we can deduce, I wonder if there’s a certain method they’re using to sort us into the twelve groups? Ayanokouji-kun, your grades are very similar to Karuizawa-san’s. Are they grouping us based on our scores? Oh, but Yukimura-kun has high academic ability, and so does Kouenji-kun. They’re at the top of the class,” said Horikita.
[says] (p.66) “No, we weren’t chosen. I’m not sure if we should be happy or sad.”
[says] (p.66) “Even so, I don’t like how they wrote it. It’s like they’re saying I’m not qualified to be the VIP.”
[says] (p.67) “I look forward to your results,” said Horikita.
[says] (p.67) “For the sake of reference, who are you most wary of? Based on how things have been going, I wanted your thoughts,” said Horikita.
[says] (p.67) “That was fast.” (on his instant “Ryuuen”)
[says] (p.67) “What about Katsuragi-kun? It’s because of him that Class A managed to quickly hold down that prime spot on the island. Isn’t he someone worth watching?”
[says] (p.68) “I’m curious about some things concerning the VIP’s identity. Even after reading the message and thinking about it, did you notice any unnatural-sounding phrases in the school’s email? And the strict—” Horikita said. (cut off by Ayanokōji’s finger)
[says] (p.68) “I’ve warned you not to call me by my first name, Ryuuen-kun. Also, considering you were a turncoat who only pretended to be our friend, it’s odd you’d show your face, Ibuki-san.”
[says] (p.68) “As if I would tell you. Perhaps you’d like to tell us about your message?” Horikita responded.
[says] (p.69) “I have nothing to tell you,” Horikita responded.
[says] (p.69) “Regrettably, I must decline your offer. Violent actions are considered a violation of the test rules. If you do happen to strike me, I will not hesitate to report it to the school. At any rate, please feel free to do whatever you please,” she said flatly.
[says] (p.69) “Since we’re all together, how about we have some coffee? It seems a good time to enjoy a nice cup,” Horikita offered.
[trait] (p.69) Narration credits her acting: she never shakes, and probably does not think of it as acting.
[says] (p.70) “Well, that’s understandable. He couldn’t have guessed that someone from Class D would do so well. Isn’t that why you and Ibuki-san are here? You came to check on me. Am I wrong?” asked Horikita.
[says] (p.70) “Of course,” Horikita answered, sipping her coffee. She seemed at ease, which was unusual.
[says] (p.72) “Think whatever you like. Though I wonder…how did you figure out my strategy? You only saw the results of the test. How do you know the way in which points were gained and lost? Those details are supposed to be unknown,” countered Horikita.
[says] (p.72) “Do you think that was hard to deduce? Any idiot could have figured that out if they thought about it for a minute. Even Katsuragi-kun, who you’ve been making fun of,” answered Horikita.
[says] (pp.72–73) “Is it possible that I’d simply taken the proper precautions? Preparing for the unknown is absolutely basic logic. The moment Ibuki-san met Class D, I took all possibilities into account. You were so confident, but your argument ended up being flimsy. You’ve said nothing surprising,” responded Horikita.
[says] (p.73) “I’m afraid I can’t understand you at all. I have no close friends. The closest I have would be Ayanokouji-kun here, and he’s always dragging me down. I’d be hard-pressed to say that he’s working with me. Sad, but true,” answered Horikita.
[trait] (p.73) Draws fire onto Ayanokōji to make him look useless — “Well done,” the narration says — while denying the second-leader theory.
[says] (p.73) “However, if we did change leaders, he wouldn’t be the most likely candidate.”
[says] (p.73-74) “Well, I’m sorry, but what you’re saying is complete nonsense. … Does it embarrass you that a girl managed to see through your … schemes?” asked Horikita.
[says] (p.74) “My goodness, that was rather thoughtful,” said Horikita.
[says] (p.74) “It sounds like you really want to believe that we have a secret weapon in our class,” observed Horikita.
[action] (p.74) Snaps when Ryūen photographs her without permission: “Don’t take my photo without permission!”
[says] (p.75) “Well, isn’t that a good thing? I don’t particularly care. Besides, if you just arbitrarily jump to conclusions, why bother badgering me to confirm them?” asked Horikita.
[says] (p.75) “Let me ask you something. I know it hurts that I saw through you, but why are you so fixated on me? Aren’t you concerned about anyone else? Like Ichinose-san, or Katsuragi-kun? Going by the rumors, there’s someone named Sakayanagi, too. Shouldn’t the people in the classes above C be your concern? You should be able to at least answer that, I think,” said Horikita.
[says] (p.75) “Not really. It’s just that I hate your sarcastic way of speaking. I’ve always aimed to reach Class A, so I assumed I’d be attracting a lot of attention,” she answered.
[says] (pp.75–76) “Really? I think he just doesn’t like that I found him out, and wanted to trick me into revealing the truth. I can’t imagine he narrowed down the list of potential candidates to you. Besides, even if he knew your identity, you’d be the only one in trouble,” replied Horikita.
[says] (p.76) “You’re overthinking things. No one thinks you’re involved. Despite what he said, he thinks you’re an ordinary person, considering your lackluster achievements in the first semester,” said Horikita.
[says] (p.76) “Discussion is likely useless from here on out. We’ll go our separate ways. Anyway, excellent work. If you make any progress, give me a report.”
[rel:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (pp.63–77) The morning partner; hands him the list, takes his wary-name, and closes with a standing report request.
[rel:ryuuen-kakeru] (pp.68–75) The full confrontation — first name, spy, island result, photo — and she never leaves the seat until he does.
[rel:ibuki-mio] (pp.68–69) Names the turncoat, declines the rematch on rules, and offers her coffee.
[rel:katsuragi-kouhei] (pp.64, 67, 70, 72–75) The dream-team anchor, the worth-watching rival, and the man Ryūen says still credits her with the island win.
[rel:hirata-yousuke] (p.64) Dragon groupmate named on her list.
[rel:kushida-kikyou] (p.64) Dragon groupmate named on her list.
[rel:sakayanagi-arisu] (p.75) The rumoured name she throws at Ryūen to break his focus.

### y1v4 p.125–133 — Ch.4 sec. 4.0: the Karuizawa brief and the planted phone
[scene] (p.125) She meets Ayanokōji face-to-face about Kouenji’s traitor exit, then argues Karuizawa’s worth while Ryuuen’s recording phone sits under his chair.
[says] (p.125) “You’re joking, right?”
[says] (p.125) “Are you an idiot? Why didn’t you stop him? Wasn’t that your responsibility as his roommate?” she asked.
[says] (p.125) “When I see him next time, I’ll reprimand him myself. I’ll make him beg for mercy.”
[says] (p.125) “You already know that’s pointless, right? He won’t listen. He’ll just confuse you and waste your time. It’ll be a headache. For the time being, we should concentrate on our own groups.”
[rel:kouenji-rokusuke] (p.125) Blames Ayanokōji as Kouenji’s roommate; accepts the roommate-can’t-stop-him answer.
[says] (p.126) “You want to talk to me about Karuizawa?”
[says] (p.126) “I turned her down, though. You know that. I have no interest in Karuizawa-san. If you want information on her, why not talk to Hirata-kun? If he’s helping you, you’ll be able to contact her easily.”
[says] (p.126) “Do you think she’s the VIP? Is that what you’re worried about?” Horikita asked.
[says] (p.126) “Well, isn’t that none of your business? Besides, her behavior isn’t logical. I think it’s a waste of time,” she countered.
[says] (p.126) “Write them off? What are you talking about?”
[says] (p.126) “She has strong points? I can’t really imagine any. Isn’t she just flawed?”
[rel:karuizawa-kei] (pp.126-127) Refuses the lunch invite history, calls her illogical, then listens to the outer/inner-self lecture and concedes time-together may help.
[says] (p.127) “So Karuizawa has some secret self?”
[says] (p.127) “I’m still not entirely convinced. However, I imagine you’ll learn more about her by spending time together,” she said.
[says] (p.127) “So, what are Karuizawa-san’s good points?” Horikita asked.
[says] (p.127) “Okay. Let’s say for the sake of argument that you’re right, and she has that ability. What do you intend to do? Are you thinking of making her your ally, too?” asked Horikita.
[rel:ryuuen-kakeru] (pp.128-131) The shade meeting: refuses to share plans, calls his no-support claim out, snaps at the phone-seizure idea, then works the planted-phone cue with Ayanokōji.
[says] (p.128) “It seems like you have an awful lot of free time. Not that I care, but there’s nothing for you here,” said Horikita.

### y1v5 p.153–156 — Ch.7 sec. 7.1: the million-point deal
[scene] (p.153) Ryuuen offers to drop the complaint for a million points, then adds a kneeling condition after Horikita first refuses.

[says:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.153) “I don’t have the time to drag this out,”
[says:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.153) “We can strike a quick deal, though.”
[says:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.153) “You should compensate Kinoshita and Class C for any losses.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.153) “This isn’t a joke. I don’t have to listen to this,”
[says:kushida-kikyou] (p.153) “What exactly do you want, then?”

[says:horikita-suzune] (p.153) “Don’t be ridiculous,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.153) “I haven’t done anything wrong. I don’t need to pay one single point.”
[says:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.153) “Then go ahead and prove that, Suzune. Lay it all out in black and white for us.”
[says:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.153) “We can prove that we aren’t lying. Let’s hurry up and have the student council president render his judgment,”

[says:kushida-kikyou] (p.154) “I don’t believe Horikita-san would do something like this on purpose.”
[says:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.154) “Oh, that’s quaint. Sorry—using your points is a no-go.”

[says:horikita-suzune] (p.155) “If I pay you, you’ll make it so this issue never happened, right?”
[says:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.155) “You’re admitting to foul play?”

[says:horikita-suzune] (p.155) “Your strategy beat mine. That’s why,”

[says:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.155) “How about you get down on your knees and beg?”
[says:chabashira-sae] (p.155) “Ryuuen. This is going too far.”
[says:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.155) “Teachers should stay out of this. It’s a problem between students,”
[says:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.155) “Kushida, bring Suzune to me after the festival.”

### y1v5 p.176–179 — Ch.8 sec. 8.3: the ally
[scene] (p.176) After an hour at the elevator, Sudou returns; Horikita admits she is worthless and asks for his strength until graduation.

[says:horikita-suzune] (p.178) “He’s my older brother.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.178) “That’s why I’ve been running straight ahead, not caring what anyone else thinks of me.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.178) “I’m not incredible. Ryuuen-kun’s thoroughly beaten me, and I haven’t gotten one single result I’m satisfied with, either.”

[says:sudou-ken] (p.178) “That sounds like a painful path to take.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.179) “If you ever stray from your path in future, I’ll bring you back to it. So, until we graduate, please lend me your strength. I promise that I’ll lend you all mine, too.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.179) “I’ll ask you again, Sudou-kun. Lend me your strength.”
[says:sudou-ken] (p.179) “I’ll work with you, Horikita. I…I feel like someone’s finally noticed me outside basketball.”
[rel:sudou-ken] (p.179) First ally: not Ayanokouji, not her brother — something she had lacked.

### y1v5 p.189–193 — Ch.9 sec. 9.1: the first-name bargain

[says:horikita-suzune] (p.189) “That still doesn’t explain it, though. That speed. You liar.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.189) “If you’d run like that from the very beginning, this situation would’ve been different. But now you’re going to be basking in lots of attention,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.191) “You didn’t take the top spot for our school year, Sudou-kun. You remember our promise, right?”
[says:sudou-ken] (p.191) “Yeah,” Sudou replied. “It’s unfortunate. But a promise is a promise. I’ll call you Horikita from now on.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.191) “I forgot to tell you one thing, though. You pushed those conditions on me, so they were rather one-sided. I never mentioned whether I had any conditions of my own.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.191) “If you took first place, you could have used my first name. Isn’t it only natural that I make a request of you in return?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.191) “You are forbidden from ever using violence without a justifiable reason ever again. Can you promise me that?”
[rel:sudou-ken] (p.191–192) Penalty bargain: he keeps the first name only if she also forgives herself — she grants “Suzune” as her own punishment.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.192) “Be that as it may, I can’t forgive myself. I need to be punished, too,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.192) “Even though we did get last place, I have hope for the battles to come, thanks to you. I’m truly grateful,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.193) “This time, we were defeated soundly. Completely.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.193) “But I feel like I grew today. I never imagined a day would come when failure would nourish me, but…that’s really how I feel.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.193) “This class is going to get stronger. Then we’ll definitely climb the ranks to the upper classes,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.193) “But I suppose, first of all, I have to get down on my knees.”

### y1v5 p.194–201 — Ch.9 sec. 9.2–9.3: the confrontation
[scene] (p.194) Horikita stays behind after the festival, waits for Ryuuen’s appointment with Kushida as escort, and faces them both in the hallway.

[says:horikita-suzune] (p.200) “I understand… I admit…”

[says:horikita-suzune] (p.45) “Some students said that the classroom was in complete disarray right after the incident. The blackboard and desks were covered with all kinds of slanderous scribblings.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.45) “I don’t know. So many rumors floated around. Someone was being bullied, or they were doing the bullying. [says:horikita-suzune] (p.47) “I can’t say for sure. Hypothetically speaking, I couldn’t overcome some people in our class with violence. I can’t imagine defeating Sudou-kun or Kouenji-kun with my bare hands. Besides, there are also people like you whose strengths I don’t fully know”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.47) “I can’t see Kushida-san using much violence. So, if the options are violence or lies, it’s only natural to conclude that she lied to destroy her class”

She won’t stop as long as I’m here, even if Class D suffers”

### y1v6 p.48–51 — Ch.3 sec. 3.1: refuse to abandon, then the Ryuuen thanks
[rel:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.48–49) Her plan is to talk Kushida down; Ayanokouji floats expulsion; she refuses and owns it as her responsibility.

And I understood. We couldn’t turn our backs on a person with something to offer. [says:horikita-suzune] (p.49) “She’s an excellent student. Not only can she win people’s hearts and minds, she’s also a keen observer. If we make her our ally, she’ll be a great asset.”

[says:horikita-suzune] (p.50) “It can’t have been easy to get that recording. How did you do it? Ryuuen-kun said there was a spy, but you’re not friends with anyone from Class C, are you?”

[rel:karuizawa-kei] (p.50) Narration: she does not know about Manabe’s cruise-ship harassment of Karuizawa.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.50) “Are you sure you’re okay with being this conspicuous? Ryuuen-kun is now convinced that someone in Class D is working behind the scenes, and you’re probably on his list, Ayanokouji-kun. I think the peaceful life you wanted is in jeopardy”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.51) “I’m starting to understand you less and less”
[rel:sakayanagi-arisu] (p.51) Ayanokouji’s inner note: Sakayanagi knows his past; peace was never a full option.

### y1v6 p.76–88 — Ch.5 sec. 5.0–5.2: apology, pairing strategy, study plan

Similarly, we’ll divide the remaining twenty students in the class into two groups of ten. The higher-scoring of those two groups should aim to score a maximum of eighty points, while the lower-scoring group only needs to score one point. By doing things this way, we automatically achieve a good balance in our pairings,”

[says:horikita-suzune] (p.119) “Do you plan on dropping out, too?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.119) “I have no intention of dropping out,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.120) “You might be right about that. I’ve been trying to come up with a way we can coexist. But I concluded that, no matter what I do, it’s impossible.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.120) “We aren’t children. We can’t just fight. But you don’t trust me.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.120) “If I score higher than you on the upcoming final exam, you’ll cooperate with me from now on without any hostility. I’m not expecting us to be best friends; I just want you to stop trying to hurt me. That’s all.”

### y1v6 p.157, 163–167 — Ch.6 sec. 6.4, sec. 6.6: prep call and the juice dump
[scene] (p.157) Calls at ten about finishing exam questions; next day chairs the karaoke study meeting and adjudicates Karuizawa’s attack on Kushida.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.157) “The library study groups are just about finished. After tomorrow’s session, I’d like to make some final preparations for the exam. Can you come along with me? If you could tell Yukimura-kun as well, that’d be a big help.”

[says:horikita-suzune] (p.157) “Keisei? It seems like you and Yukimura-kun have gotten quite close. You don’t need to worry about the other two. I already talked to them,” said Horikita.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.163) “Just enjoy the food and drinks and deal with it,” said Horikita.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.163) “Okay, I’m going to start,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.164) “our study sessions. To be perfectly honest, I think the results have been fantastic. The boys were imbeciles at first, so I was worried, but fortunately, they studied hard.”

Did you manage to resolve Hasebe-san and Miyake-kun’s issues?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.165) “Can you please calm down, Karuizawa-san? We’re in the middle of discussing our final exam. Don’t waste our time,” said Horikita.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.166) “Yes, Karuizawa-san. Kushida-san hasn’t done anything bad to you,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.167) “Kushida-san, do you have a spare blazer to wear tomorrow? Will you be okay?”

### y1v6 p.177–187 — Ch.7 sec. 7.0: the preemptive counter
[scene] (p.177) At the base of the stairs with the manila envelope of questions she and Hirata spent a month building; only she knows all of them.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.177) “Thinking about something?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.177) “Hard to say. Don’t expect too much. The school made many”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.178) “Is this a coincidence? Or an ambush, perhaps?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.178) “You’re not worried about any traitors in your own class?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.178) “Yet you waited until the last minute to submit your questions,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.178) “Ayanokouji-kun, have you been studying properly? How are things with you and your partner?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.178) “Thinking isn’t enough. We can’t afford to have a single student drop out. Don’t get complacent, even though I’m sure we can handle whatever Class C throws at us,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.179) “Hmm, who knows? Maybe it was simply cheap provocation? Just your style,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.179) “These are the final questions, Chabashira-sensei,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.179) “I’d like to ask you one thing, if I may,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.179) “These questions are linked to either Class D’s victory or defeat. They’re two sides of the same coin. We must avoid a leak at all costs. After I hand these papers over to you, could you please not show them to anyone else? Myself included.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.179) “Would that be a problem?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.180) “I didn’t receive permission from every individual student, but…I don’t think they’d object. No one wants their class to lose,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.180) “Are you asking me to prove that? You want me to show the questions to everyone in class and confirm that they’re okay with them?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.180) “Thank you very much. Now I can face the exam in peace.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.180) “A painful lesson to learn. I’ll work harder to bring the class together,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.180) “Some things can’t stay the same.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.180) “Yes, as long as I’m present at the time,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.181) “Chabashira-sensei. You said that you wouldn’t lie, didn’t you?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.181) “In that case, will the school accept the questions I just submitted to you?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.181) “Let me rephrase that. Have you already accepted other test questions?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.181) “I’d like to hear the answer from your own lips, Chabashira-sensei.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.182) “So…does that mean someone else already submitted test questions and answers?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.182) “Please cancel acceptance of the previously submitted test questions. I have the correct ones here,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.182) “What in the world?” The fight drained out of Horikita, and she slumped over. This was far too cruel. “What student? Please tell me.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.182) “But under the right circumstances, Class D can change the questions the school already accepted, yes?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.183) “How much time do we have left to correct the questions?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.183) “Were you behind this? Did you instruct Kushida-san to do this?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.183) “I’m not going to tolerate this outsider eavesdropping on the conversation any further!”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.183) “Did Kushida-san instruct you not to show the questions to anyone?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.183) “Please show them to me, then.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.183) “These are incredibly difficult,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.184) “So, is there nothing more to be done?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.184) “Sorry. I wanted to take extra precautions just in case, so I kept up the act,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.184) “Yes. I couldn’t allow myself to be defeated the same way I was at the sports festival. When the final exam’s details were announced, I immediately consulted Chabashira-sensei. I had two requests. One, that I alone submit Class D’s usable test questions, and two, that Chabashira-sensei pretend to accept any other questions submitted,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.185) “Now that they definitely believe that Kushida’s test problems were used, if any Class C students aren’t studying for the test, they may just fail,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.187) “I understand. However, this ends with the final exam.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.187) “Sorry for keeping quiet about this.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.187) “Ryuuen’s knocked me down so many times now. I thought that it was about time I learned,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.187) “All that’s left now is to beat Kushida-san’s exam score.”
[feat] (p.179–185) Preemptively consulted Chabashira: she alone submits usable questions, teacher pretends to accept Kushida’s — a counter to the sports-festival playbook Ayanokōji calls something he could not have devised.

[rel:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.178–183) Ambush walk, mockery, and expulsion from the corridor — she will not tolerate the outsider eavesdropping.

[rel:miyake-akito] (p.185) Named with Haruka as the hardest-hit average students.

### y1v6 p.194–199 — Ch.7 sec. 7.3: the score claim and the bet settled
[scene] (p.194) After the first exam day, claims ninety-eight or higher in math; walks Kushida to the special building to settle the bet.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.194) “A hundred. Or, at least, that’s what I’d like to say. Since there was one part where the question seemed ambiguous, I can probably only say I got ninety-eight points for certain. A few reasonably difficult questions were mixed in.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.194) “None. I’m certain that I overcame this test. I think I managed to get near-perfect scores in the other three areas as well.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.194) “I made this bet with Kushida-san assuming that she’d score a hundred points. I was incredibly thorough in my preparations so I wouldn’t suffer even a trivial mistake. Still, it’s a shame that I might’ve missed two points in the end,”

[says:horikita-suzune] (p.195) “That’s not terrible. Considering what was on the test, you did well,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.195) “I have a little something to take care of, so head on back with your friends.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.195) “My business has nothing to do with him. I’m meeting Kushida-san. Is there a problem?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.195) “All right. But, considering what’s coming tomorrow, make sure to go to bed early,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.195) “It’s not a big deal. Since we can estimate our respective scores, I just want to confirm something with her,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.195) “What’s the matter with her, I wonder?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.195) “I hope so. Ryuuen can be rather tenacious.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.196) “Was that sarcasm?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.196) “Nothing, forget it.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.196) “Do you have a moment? I’d like to confirm something with you. But there are people around, so how about we go somewhere else?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.196) “Ayanokouji-kun will come along, since he’s involved in this matter. You don’t mind, do you?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.196) “Yes. Though the results haven’t been announced yet, we should be able to estimate our scores well enough ourselves,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.196) “I’m confident that I scored a ninety-eight or higher. What about you?”

[says:horikita-suzune] (p.197) “No need. Let’s just start by trusting each other,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.197) “I know. But I think I can work hard to change that,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.199) “I see. That’s a shame,”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.199) “I should’ve thought this through more carefully,”
