### y2v10 p.31, 40–41, 43–50 — Ch.2: The leader question, asked and answered
[scene] (p.31) She received hints from senpais and was already more concerned than the class; her demeanor gives nothing away.
[scene] (p.40) Horikita raises her hand — "may I ask a question?" — to probe the leader-elimination mechanics.
[says] (p.40) “Excuse me, may I ask a question?”
[says] (p.40) “What happens if the leader is eliminated midway through the special exam? Also, will those who are eliminated be required to do something like leaving the room?”
[says] (p.41) “The leader only directs and doesn’t fight...”
[scene] (p.43) Without her speaking yet, the class naturally gathers around her after the homeroom; she listens before claiming.
[scene] (p.44) She gets the floor; her stated fear honestly named, her answer to it: do not become overly anxious.
[says] (p.44) “Just hearing this, naturally, you’d want to avoid elimination at all costs. However, what I really want to say is to avoid becoming overly anxious. While we are still unclear about the essence of the special exam, let’s first start by unifying our overall awareness without causing a fuss.”
[scene] (p.44) She decides to gather the class at lunch; participation encouraged but not mandatory.
[scene] (p.45) Ten minutes into lunch, 37 classmates, Kōenji absent; Yōsuke asks her in front of everyone.
[says] (p.45) “If I’m nominated by many, I have no intention of refusing. But in this special exam, while the leader carries great responsibility, there’s also a rule that excuses them from the risk of expulsion or leaving school. If there are other potential candidates, I would like to listen to their ideas.”
[scene] (p.46) She moves to the podium; thirty seconds of silence; no one volunteers; she intends to take the role regardless but keeps her reservations formal.
[says] (p.46) “Is there anyone here who wants to be the leader? If so, please tell us.”
[says] (p.46) “Yes, of course, I intend to. I showed a reserved attitude to hear other people’s opinions, but I always intended to take on the role of the leader. If there are no objections...”
[scene] (p.47) Maezono demands a discussion on Horikita's decisiveness; the unanimous-vote flashback; Hasebe clouds; Horikita concedes the hesitation publicly.
[says] (p.48) “I see... that’s a hard truth. Indeed, at that time, I hesitated. I refused to follow the wishes of the class majority and made a personal decision. There’s no denying that it’s a fact.”
[says] (p.48) “I know that I have many immature aspects. I can’t declare that I’m the best choice for the leader. Nevertheless, right now, no one else is stepping up to take the role.”
[says] (p.48) “I see—a recommendation. Of course, there might be some in the class who think someone else would be better than me. But I have already asked the class once. If there was a student who wanted to be a leader, they would have raised their hand. Is it okay to leave the decision to someone who doesn’t nominate themselves?”
[says] (p.48) “Or should we ask Kōenji-kun, who is the only one who hasn’t participated in this discussion? He has a sharp edge and can undoubtedly make decisions.”
[scene] (p.48–49) Her counter-offer is a trap she is willing to spring: anyone better can have it.
[says] (p.49) “Your thinking is correct too. I agree with the opinion that we should search for someone stronger and faster in good decision-making. So after listening to what you just said, I am asking the whole class. In this special exam, students who are confident that they can lead and guide the class to victory, please raise your hand. If someone emerges who I think is more suitable than me, I would gladly give up the leadership role.”
[note] (p.49) The hand-raise challenge is clearly aimed at Ayanokōji; no one moves; she understood he would not volunteer.
[scene] (p.50) Sudō's question lands; she answers with the email — polite, written, expulsion-protection demand.
[says] (p.50) “A timely topic. I just received a polite email that said, ‘It goes without saying, but I’d be in trouble if you don’t protect me from expulsion.’”
[says] (p.50) “Don’t jump to conclusions. We can’t say for certain that we need to constantly protect him. We’ll think of some countermeasures. I won’t discuss it in detail now, but don’t worry too much.”
[note] (p.50) She flags Kōenji-strategy talk as off-limits for public spaces and phones, and commits to no details.
[rel:maezono] (p.46–49) Faces her challenge head-on; answers it with the class ask; Maezono withdraws.
[rel:hirata-yousuke] (p.45–50) He backs her from the first pre-lunch question to the end; they alternate explaining and eating.
[trait] (p.31) Received hints from senpais; more concerned than the class; keeps it off her face.


### y2v10 p.81–89 — Ch.4: The karaoke; the 68-point autopsy
[scene] (p.81) Wednesday afternoon, two days before the exam; Ike stands up with his winning strategy; she responds seriously but shuts down on-the-spot strategy talk.
[says] (p.81) “Ike-kun. I will respond seriously for now, but I don’t want to discuss strategy here. Understand?”
[says] (p.81) “That sounds good. Those who are interested can come with us, however, keep in mind that it’s a hassle if there are too many people. Can you raise your hand if you would like to come along?”
[note] (p.81) Speaker of the "That sounds good..." line: Horikita answers Yōsuke's suggestion by turn-taking; untagged in the print.
[scene] (p.82) Karaoke room; she finishes ordering for everyone; the plum kelp tea arrives scalding — AK apologizes.
[says] (p.83) “Ahem, let’s hear Ike-kun’s idea.”
[scene] (p.83) She engages Ike with full seriousness; her expression shows no amusement; his face goes tense.
[says] (p.83) “68 points? Sure, if we had 68 points, there would indeed be a good chance of victory, but that seems like a very specific score.”
[scene] (p.83) She sees the arithmetic instantly: 32 eliminations → 68 guaranteed; she stays keenly listening.
[says] (p.84) “That’s a great idea for you. It’s certainly a strategy that could maintain a high average score.”
[says] (p.85) “If it weren’t for ‘a certain rule’ in this special exam, this might’ve remained a candidate that we could have implemented.”
[says] (p.85) “Let’s say, hypothetically, that Ryūen-kun’s class implements this strategy right after the exam starts.”
[says] (p.85) “Even though there was one person expelled from his class, the addition of Katsuragi-kun has kept their numbers at 40. Except the leader and five others, there would be 34 eliminations. This means they would secure 66 points. While that certainly isn’t a bad score, it also means they can’t possibly score any higher. If the remaining three classes each manage to score 67 points or more, that approach becomes a ‘no-win’ strategy.”
[says] (p.85) “No. Most likely, if Ryūen-kun’s class were to use that strategy, they would end up in last place.”
[says] (p.86) “Listen. If they’re going to use the faking sickness strategy to cause mass elimination, they should naturally do that in the first turn. There’s little merit in implementing this strategy after the second turn.”
[says] (p.86) “Moreover, this strategy is conspicuous. All three classes would quickly find out. Just imagine their class situation when the classes see through that strategy. Will they think, ‘Oh no, they’ve played a good move?’”
[says] (p.86) “No. On the contrary. If they play that strategy, it would actually make things easier for the other three classes.”
[says] (p.86) “Yes. So as soon as we see their aim, we would use this to cooperate with the other classes. If Ryūen-kun’s class could only get up to 66 points, the other three classes should team up and aim for more. If they judge that a losing class has appeared, Ichinose-san and Sakayanagi-san will also positively consider this option.”
[says] (p.86) “They’d lose. Who to nominate, who to protect. Just by doing this, the two classes Ryūen-kun’s class targets would definitely get 50 points. Hence, they would only need to earn 17 more points. Under the current rules, they can raise the difficulty by using points, but if the score is zero or less, they can only attack with the base difficulty. Therefore, it wouldn’t be difficult to score more than 17 points.”
[scene] (p.86) She shows him her phone during the explanation — the coordination channel is ready.
[says] (p.87) “You don’t have to apologize. Your strategy was well thought out. What I need to apologize for is for assuming from the start that it definitely wouldn’t be helpful.”
[says] (p.87) “Your strategy has a chance of winning. If the other three classes can’t cooperate, the chances of winning would increase. Even if they gang up on us, there’s still a chance of winning. For lower-ability classes, it’s not a bad idea to pin their hopes on this strategy. However, I believe our class has the ability to fight without depending on that method.”
[says] (p.87) “It became clear that this special exam would be troublesome if the others gang up on us.”
[says] (p.87) “You were brave to speak up.”
[says] (p.88) “Shinohara-san, Hondō-kun, and Miyamoto-kun, too. Please feel free to voice any ideas you may have. Also, relay this to classmates who aren’t present here. I promise not to look down on anyone from the get-go.”
[scene] (p.88–89) The others leave smiling; AK proposes a change of location; she jokes about the Karuizawa trouble; he asks for the one-on-one.
[says] (p.88) “I’m going home. Until yesterday, I was meeting Hirata-kun and the others here every day, but it was a day off to rest.”
[says] (p.89) “I don’t have any plans after this, but it’s unusual for you to want to talk. Unless it’s about trouble with Karuizawa-san, in which case I’d like to decline.”
[says] (p.89) “You? About the special exam?”
[says] (p.89) “I see. You want to assess if I’m prepared to fight well?”
[says] (p.89) “Would I be self-conscious if I said I’m worried about others spying and listening in?”
[says] (p.89) “Well, as long as you say so, there won’t be any problem. Shall we get going right away then?”

### y2v10 p.90–100 — Ch.4: The café; the Ibuki hypothesis
[scene] (p.90) Weekday café; two blended coffees; the window seat; he studies her profile — the fatigue of constant thinking; the glare at being stared at.
[says] (p.90) “Thank you. The money—”
[says] (p.90) “Well then, I’ll gladly accept your offer.”
[says] (p.90) “...Is there something on my face?”
[scene] (p.91) She ran her fingers through her hair — almost a year since she cut it short; she teases him about the spilled-coffee threat; the Ryūen orange-juice memory surfaces.
[says] (p.91) “It’s been almost a year since I cut it short. Time flies, doesn’t it?”
[says] (p.91) “What would happen if a tragic accident occurred here, and I grabbed you and poured hot coffee directly into your shirt?”
[says] (p.91) “You would dodge it if I tried to spill the contents all over you here, right?”
[says] (p.91) “Why are you trying to move seats? I would never do such a thing. I wouldn’t cause so much trouble for the store.”
[says] (p.91) “Please prioritize not causing major burns to a classmate instead.”
[says] (p.91) “Really... you’re such a strange one, aren’t you?”
[says] (p.91) “I’m not weird. I just... sometimes my seriousness is misplaced.”
[scene] (p.92) The mindset talk; her post-unanimous-vote self-questioning; she steadies her posture.
[says] (p.92) “...Um, I’m sorry, I’m not quite sure what you mean by ‘mindset.’”
[says] (p.92) “Should I assume that you’ll correct me if you think my mindset is wrong?”
[says] (p.92) “That’s suspicious.”
[says] (p.92) “Am I really that suspicious?”
[says] (p.92) “I mean, you being concerned about me is, well, kind of creepy, don’t you think?”
[says] (p.92) “Yeah, you’re right. Um, let me get my thoughts together.”
[says] (p.92) “I want to ask you frankly. Have you decided what to do if we come in last place in this special exam?”
[says] (p.92) “It’s a hard question to immediately answer, isn’t it?”
[says] (p.92) “Since that day, I have been questioning myself over and over again. At times, I feel guilty and regretful, even though I believe I made the right decision. It’s a depressing thought.”
[says] (p.92) “I can’t say for sure what will happen in the future. It’s not just me, everyone in the class is growing little by little every day. Even if we rank them based on their abilities, it would fluctuate.”
[says] (p.92) “But the next special exam is different. At least, I plan to face it with two options in mind in case we end up at the bottom. One is a less painful choice, the”
[says] (p.93) “other is a bitter one. However, since there are various obstacles, there’s no guarantee that the less painful choice can be realized...”
[says] (p.93) “Whichever it turns out to be, I’ve set up my own guidelines to choose without hesitation.”
[says] (p.93) “True... but... you don’t normally think about losing first...”
[says] (p.93) “...Thank you...”
[says] (p.93) “You helped me in the unanimous voting exam. Ah, is that all you wanted to know?”
[says] (p.94) “I guess so.”
[says] (p.94) “There’s no question about it. I want my class to win; there’s no other choice.”
[says] (p.94) “I’m sorry, but we prioritize our victory. That’s the right choice, isn’t it?”
[says] (p.94) “...Ibuki-san...?”
[says] (p.94) “Hold on a minute. Ibuki-san is close to Ryūen-kun. I can’t imagine she would be the first candidate for expulsion. Is this even a valid hypothesis?”
[says] (p.94) “But—”
[says] (p.95) “...For the sake of our class’s victory, it’s natural to choose victory, even if it means Ibuki-san is the specific person who has to be expelled.”
[says] (p.95) “What are you trying to say?”
[says] (p.95) “If you’re also considering ‘not close’ relationships, then yes, I won’t deny it.”
[says] (p.96) “But I don’t know what kind of battle suits me yet...”
[says] (p.96) “Well… no, I haven’t. All I was thinking about was how to minimize the damage if I lose, who to expel from our side in case of emergency, and what I would need to do to win for the class.”
[says] (p.96) “You mean... I should be ready to lose someone like Ibuki-san or someone in the same position?”
[says] (p.96) “To be honest, I don’t think I can maintain that awareness at this moment.”
[says] (p.97) “But I’ll keep repeating it to myself until the special exam. If that doesn’t work, I’ll keep telling myself even after that. I don’t know how far I can go... I’m sorry. I’m not good enough...”
[says] (p.97) “Hey—what are you, exactly?”
[says] (p.97) “That’s exactly what I mean. I don’t understand you at all...”
[says] (p.97) “At the very least, as long as I’m the appointed leader, it isn’t bad to get to know my classmates. Even for the next special exam, being aware of the details puts us in an advantageous position.”
[says] (p.97) “So, you understand Kōenji?”
[says] (p.97) “...Quite right.”
[says] (p.98) “You were uninterested in reaching Class A, and were fundamentally reserved and unsociable. But before I knew it, you started dating Karuizawasan, you started to help the class knowing that it’d make you stand out. There is no consistency in what you do.”
[says] (p.98) “I can’t see it like that. You don’t fit into any conventionally assumed categories. I’m convinced of that. There’s always a reason beyond ordinary thinking for what you do. Because...”
[says] (p.98) “...How could such a personality be born, I wonder. What kind of child were you?”

### y2v10 p.123–188 — Ch.6: The special exam; the leader's exam
[scene] (p.123) The classroom is rearranged for the exam; she takes her seat behind him; Sakagami arrives; the monitor does the talking.
[says] (p.123) “Do you feel at ease?”
[says] (p.123) “I’m sorry, but I absolutely won’t use the protection on you.”
[says] (p.123) “Shut up. I’ll stab you.”

[says] (p.126) “We’re starting while flying blind. Let’s just proceed as discussed with Hirata-kun and the others.”
[scene] (p.129) Defending, she protects five — Sonoda, Ichihashi, Okitani, Ike, Makida; Kōenji absent; Okitani and Ike successfully protected; she nets two points.
[says] (p.130) “The exam only puts you at risk of expulsion if you get eliminated. I decided he was safe until he got two questions wrong. There’s no other reason to protect him from the onset.”

[scene] (p.133) Third turn — Gourmet Food D1 again; Kōenji blanks again; Keisei raises his hand for the second time, still unprotected? no — she hints she'll protect him; she protects no one; the four others answer correctly; 10 points.
[says] (p.133) “I’m not that worried about you. But, just to be safe, if I feel like you’re being attacked in the same category again, I’m going to protect you. Alright?”
[scene] (p.135) Fourth turn — Sakayanagi spends a point on D2; she held Kōenji out of the five protected; OK shows his hand: Kōenji answers — correctly — avoiding elimination; she improvises, correctly, against the read.
[scene] (p.137–138) Kōenji confronts her; she dispenses with ceremony — she was ready for him; the exchange is short, violent, polite; he walks back to his seat.
[says] (p.137) “You’re not out. You don’t have to worry about being eliminated at this point, right?”
[says] (p.137) “Reasons? Was there a problem?”
[says] (p.137) “But you gave the correct answer.”
[says] (p.137) “If you understand, could you go back to your seat? From your height, you’re blocking my view of the monitor.”
[scene] (p.138) End of the first half — the scoreboard: 29 (Sakayanagi), 28 (her), 24 (Ichinose, zero out), 19 (Ryūen).
[scene] (p.139) Yōsuke tells her she did well; she doesn't celebrate; she keeps her target firmly on Ichinose.
[note] (p.139) “Yes, her stance hasn’t changed even in this special exam. Thanks to that, we managed to keep things under control.” — untagged; no attribution assigned; the exchange celebrates her tactic without naming it.

[note] (p.140) “Assuming she gives up protecting everyone in the second half, perhaps, but...” — untagged; attribution open.

[scene] (p.141) Lunchtime; she's cornered in the corridor; the naming-order tripwire; she plays it cool.
[says] (p.141) “I wonder if there was such a pattern... I honestly didn’t pay attention to the order. It wasn’t in alphabetical order, or boys first and then girls either, was it?”

[scene] (p.167) Turn 12, last leg — News D1; AK and Kei nominated again; he freezes on ‘tapiru’; she answers correctly; we pull four points.

[says] (p.171) “That’s true. It might be best to protect once more.”

[says] (p.185) “Admittedly, the situation is close to dire. Our class has four eliminations at this point. Ichinose-san has taken the lead, and Ryūen-kun’s class, which was in last place, is catching up at an absurd pace with continuous perfect scores. We’ve reached a point where I can’t guarantee that we’ll definitely win.”
[says] (p.187) “All we can do is fight together until the end.”
[says] (p.187) “Believe in me.”

[trait] (p.123, 140, 185) Unbreakable public face; gut-level risk calculations absorb the blows; the leader's oath overrides every personal tie.
[limit] (p.171, 185) Cannot produce a miracle; her class senses fear; she cannot guarantee anything.

### y2v10 p.200–201 — Ch.7: The debrief hallway talk
[scene] (p.200) After Ichinose leaves, AK and Horikita walk to the corridor where they can talk; she reflects on the result and what it means for her class.
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.200) “We lost this time. In the second half, it was clear that there must have been some kind of deal between Ryūen-kun’s and Ichinose-san’s classes... Of course, without any evidence, it’s just speculation, but his class gave points to Ichinose-san’s class, which elevated them to second place, without a single elimination.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.200) “Exactly, but that’s not the main point.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.200) “If two classes colluded, they basically had to start working together from the first half. Only by assisting each other could both parties benefit and share the victory. So I was fully relieved when there were no signs of it even after the first half was over.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.200) “It wasn’t just you. Sakayanagi couldn’t have expected it either.”
[note] (p.201) “But the turning point of all this was that Ryūen was able to sense all of Sakayanagi’s attack targets beforehand.” — untagged in the print; none assigned.
[note] (p.201) “Someone leaked Class A’s information to him... That’s the only explanation.” — untagged in the print; none assigned.
[note] (p.201) “That seems to be the case.” — untagged in the print; none assigned.
[note] (p.201) “That student is insane. I can’t imagine something like clearly betraying the class. This wasn’t Class D or Class C, but a class that has maintained A since enrollment. What did they get in return for executing this?” — untagged in the print; none assigned.
[note] (p.201) “They could betray their class if they were being paid 20 million points. Other than that, they probably wouldn’t do it.” — untagged in the print; none assigned.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.201) “I’m not thrilled with the outcome, but I can’t complain. Although it’s disappointing not coming in first, Class A ended up being fourth, so there’s basically no damage done. However… I still feel frustrated.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.201) “Take that frustration to the next special exam.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.201) “Okay... I’ll do that.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.201) “I’m going to check out Ryūen’s class. What are you going to do?”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.201) “I’ll go home for today. I don’t have the confidence that I can listen to his sarcasm maturely.”

### y2v11 p.91–95 — Ch.3: The debt
[scene] (p.91) She pulls Ayanokōji to the edge of the lobby; her favor is personal; she owes Amasawa a debt she cannot repay in school life.
[says] (p.91) “I'm glad I ran into you. I have a little favor to ask... Is that okay?”
[says] (p.91) “It's about Amasawa-san... It's not something I can talk about out loud.”
[says] (p.92) “Did you know how good her physical abilities are? It seems that she’s also quite skilled in martial arts.”
[says] (p.92) “Well, various things.”
[says] (p.92) “I've been doing my best every day. But I don't know if I'm at a level that can compete with her. So, I want you to evaluate my current strength.”
[says] (p.93) “It would be under normal circumstances, but her strength isn’t normal.”
[says] (p.93) “You just need to judge my strength in your own way. Of course, I would be happy if you could give me some advice.”
[says] (p.93) “Not yet.”
[says] (p.93) “If she refuses my proposal, I won't force her to do it.”
[says] (p.93) “Would you... accept my request?”
[says] (p.93) “Unfortunately, I'm tired of sparring with Ibuki-san. There's little to gain from fighting the same opponent over and over.”
[says] (p.93) “You're strong, so you should be willing to help a little.”
[says] (p.94) “It would be too conspicuous to have a fighting rematch at school.”
[says] (p.94) “I wonder... Honestly, there’s nothing in it for you...”
[says] (p.94) “But if you were to accept, I'm willing to offer private points as compensation—”
[says] (p.94) “Really? I wasn't expecting that at all...”
[says] (p.94) “Thank you. I couldn't ask for more cooperation. So, what are the conditions?”
[says] (p.94) “Of course, I understand that. What are the other conditions?”
[says] (p.95) “If I get permission from Amasawa-san, I'll send a message.”
[says] (p.95) “I don't quite understand, but I just need to keep that in mind, right?”
[says] (p.95) “I understand. It's not a big deal.”
[note] (p.92–93) The debt's stage was likely the island exam (martial arts + debt); Kiyotaka silently knows Amasawa's strength.

### y2v11 p.185–188 — Ch.8: The fight that happens off-page

[says:ibuki-mio] (p.186) “But I can knock him down with my kick!”

### y2v11 p.194–198 — Epilogue: The morning alone
[scene] (p.194) A few days after the exchange meeting; she waits alone in the cold morning; he sees her from the corner of his eye and approaches.
[says] (p.195) “Good morning, Ayanokōji-kun.”
[says] (p.195) “You're early.”
[says] (p.195) “In a way? That's a scary way to put it.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.195) “I often talk to you about special exams and class-related matters, but sometimes I want to talk about things that have nothing to do with that.”
[says] (p.195) “Forever is an exaggeration. Sure, that's true, but we still have over a year until graduation, right? We don't have to arrange meetings like this, I can chat anytime.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.195) “What if I were to be expelled at the end-of-year exam?”
[says] (p.195) “That's a jump. I don't think you'll be expelled. But seeing how easily you get common sense questions wrong, maybe there's a possibility...”
[says] (p.195) “Are you really worried that you might be expelled? Is that why you wanted to talk so early in the morning...?”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.195) “The last special exam has become a bit of a trauma for me.”
[says] (p.196) “Then why don't you try to remember more common sense questions? You're good at studying.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.196) “So, can you memorize game and anime terms as effectively as studying for other subjects?”
[says] (p.196) “Don't worry. From a class perspective, your existence is essential. Even if you struggle with common sense questions, I'll always back you up. In other words, you won't be expelled.”
[says] (p.196) “Actually, I'm not worried about myself, but about the possibility of your expulsion.”
[says] (p.196) “In reality, that seems more likely.”
[says] (p.196) “That many?”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.196) “To comply with the government's policy, they'll make the rules more strict?”
[says] (p.196) “Last year, because there were no dropouts, it was forcibly changed from zero to one expulsion. I wouldn't be surprised if several people will get expelled in the upcoming end-of-year exam.”
[says] (p.196) “Aren't you overthinking...?”
[says] (p.196) “In that case, I guess so. I'd like you to work hard too.”
[says] (p.196) “That's a rather unusual answer for you. Between the special training and now, you've been a bit too cooperative lately. Even with the Amasawa-san situation, you didn't look displeased even once.”
[says] (p.197) “I heard about the results from Kushida, it seems you fought well, but you lost.”
[says] (p.197) “She's really strong, isn't she? Even if we fought two against one, we still couldn't win in the end.”
[says] (p.197) “You should be able to have a better fight next time.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.197) “Ibuki-san said she wouldn't team up with me again.”
[says] (p.197) “It's fine. She forgets things quickly.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.198) “She's my ex-girlfriend.”
[says:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.198) “It was a bad joke.”
[says] (p.198) “Someday, I want to hear the truth from your own mouth, Ayanokōji-kun.”
[says] (p.198) “I'm not.”
[note] (p.194–198) She expects several dropouts in the end-of-year exam; the government's annual 10-dropout target; she promises she'll always back him; she laughs at the bad ex-girlfriend joke; the conversation never stops being about the exam.
[rel:ayanokouji-kiyotaka] (p.194–198) The morning walk; the exam stakes; the promise of backup; the bad joke.
